## 2026-11-02 -- Release v0.1.1

Second release and the first through the full release pipeline. It is the update target for 0.1.0 package installs (`arco update` or UPDATE AND RESTART) and a fresh-install target on Windows. User-visible changes: messages sent mid-turn show Queued or Delivered and the working indicator names the current step with its elapsed time; cluster coordination treats silence as approval of a stated next step, send results show the recipient's activity, and status-only director messages are held once for confirmation; new homes enable only signed-in providers and detected local runtimes; Claude status follows the selected transport and the Claude sign-ins are grouped in Settings; project locations that are Git repositories list themselves and their child folders; the usage dropdowns are reduced to the provider name and usage bars; `llama-server` is found on PATH; a package uninstall path is documented. On Windows the background service runs without a console window through `wscript.exe` and the `service/launch.js` launcher in the Arco home, its output goes to log files shown by `arco service logs`, and stop, restart and uninstall end the whole service process tree.

Release controls: `scripts/release.mjs patch` bumps the four manifests and the lockfile to 0.1.1, moves Unreleased into the `v0.1.1` section and creates this commit and the annotated tag. `.github/workflows/release.yml` builds the native archives, attaches `arco-release.tgz` to the GitHub Release, and the npm job stages the package through trusted publishing in the `npm` environment for approval on the registry.

Checked: on the release base, `npm ci`, build, typecheck, 1289 unit tests, the tree audit, `docs:check` and all 37 browser suites passed locally; hosted CI passed verify, package install and browser suites on Linux and Windows, verify and package install on macOS, all six native package builds and the Docker smoke. Two Windows defects were found by hosted CI before the tag: `conhost.exe --headless` started no child process, and ending the task left Node running; both were fixed and proven on the Windows runner.

Remaining: on a real Windows desktop, confirm that no window appears, and exercise restart and uninstall through Task Scheduler; the first trusted-publisher run and the registry approval.

### Added

- Document a complete package uninstall path (service removal, npm uninstall, optional permanent data and checkpoint cleanup) in Operations, with a README link and in-app manual entry.
- Show mid-turn message delivery and the current timed working step in workflows and cluster cards.

### Changed

- Cluster coordination treats silence as approval of a stated next step and asks continuing reports to name that step.
- Peer send results and mailbox inspection show recipient activity, current task and latest reply to the sender.
- Director status-only peer messages ask for confirmation before delivery; reports and actionable messages continue normally.
- New homes enable detected provider sign-ins and local runtimes; Strata and DeepSeek through Xolotl start off. Existing provider choices remain intact.
- The usage dropdowns on the provider dots are minimal: the provider name, then the usage bars with percent and reset time (Claude Session and Weekly, Codex its usage lines, Cursor what its source provides), with one small refresh button. Sign-in and account detail stays in Connections and Settings. "Allowance" wording is now "usage limits".

### Fixed

- Project locations that are Git repositories now show the repository and its child folders; workflow names use the selected project display name.
- Windows: the background service no longer leaves a console window open on the desktop. The task now starts Node through a hidden script host launcher, raw output is kept in log files under the Arco home, `arco service logs` shows them, and `arco update` and UPDATE AND RESTART move an older install to the new task definition. Stopping or restarting the Windows service now also ends the Node process, so the port is freed.
- Claude: Welcome and Connections now report Claude as connected only when the selected transport can be used. With Native (the default) selected and no Native credentials, Claude reads "Native: sign-in needed" with SIGN IN FOR NATIVE in place and "Use Claude CLI instead" when the CLI is signed in, and Continue waits for a usable sign-in.
- Settings > Providers and models groups the Claude sign-ins together (Native first, then CLI) ahead of the other accounts and local endpoints, and the link from Connections lands on that group and highlights it.
- Repeated ADD FRESH batches with the same name continue numbering after existing cluster members.
- Fresh service installation on Windows and macOS no longer prints the expected missing-service probe as an error.
- llama.cpp: `llama-server` is now found on PATH on every platform (`llama-server.exe` on Windows) instead of a fixed Linux path, `ARCO_LLAMACPP_BIN` still overrides it, and the not-found message says where to look.

## 2026-11-02 -- Windows service stop now ends the Node process, not only the launcher

With the script host launcher, the scheduled task's process is `wscript.exe`
and Node is its child. A probe on the hosted Windows runner showed that
`taskkill /PID <wscript> /F` leaves Node running, and that registering the real
task definition, running it and ending it with `schtasks /End` also left Node
alive. Left alone, `arco service stop` would not stop Arco, a restart would hit
the held port, and `arco update` would refuse because Arco processes were still
running. `serviceAction('stop')` in `scripts/packaging/services.mjs`, which
`restart` and `uninstall` also use, now ends the task and then runs a short
PowerShell step that selects `wscript.exe` and `node.exe` processes whose
command line contains this home's `service/config.json` and ends each with
`taskkill /T /F`. The marker is the per-home configuration path, so another
home's Arco and a source-checkout service are not matched. The update guard is
unchanged and stays the final check. A unit test covers the order and the
failure path. `scripts/packaging/pack-smoke.mjs` starts a stand-in service
through the written launcher, kills only the launcher as `/End` does, runs the
real stop code, and requires both processes gone and the port free. Checked on
Linux with the unit suite and gate; the Windows result comes from the hosted
pull request run. Not verified: a real Windows desktop.

---

## 2026-11-02 -- Windowless Windows service moved from conhost to a script host launcher

Hosted CI on the windowless Windows service change failed two Windows jobs, and
the second showed the launcher itself was wrong. The package update unit test
(`scripts/packaging/update.test.mjs`) expected npm calls `view` and `install`;
on win32 the update also runs `arco service refresh` in a fresh process, so the
expectation is now platform-aware and asserts that third `service refresh`
call. The pack-install smoke (`scripts/packaging/pack-smoke.mjs`) reported that
`conhost.exe --headless node` wrote no marker. A throwaway probe workflow on the
Windows runner then tried seven variants (piped and ignored stdio, quoted and
verbatim command lines, `cmd /c`, no `--headless`, a bare `node`): conhost
exited with status 0 and started no child in every one, even after waiting.
The same probe showed `wscript.exe //B //Nologo launch.js <node> <script>` and
PowerShell `-WindowStyle Hidden` both run node, including from a path with
spaces. The task now runs `wscript.exe` (a GUI-subsystem program, so no console
window) with a JScript launcher, `service/launch.js` in the Arco home, that
runs node hidden and waits. `services.mjs` writes it at install and at
`arco service refresh`, and removes it on uninstall; JScript is used because
VBScript is being retired. The smoke now runs the written launcher against a
spaced path and requires the marker. Checked on Linux: build, typecheck, unit
and audits. Not verified: that no window appears on a real desktop, and task
stop kills the whole tree; both need a real Windows host.

## 2026-11-02 -- Package uninstall procedure in Operations

Users can now follow a complete removal path for a packaged native install on
Linux, macOS, and Windows. The steps cover stopping the per-user service,
removing the npm package, optionally deleting Arco data with an explicit
permanent-loss warning, and stating what remains installed.

The procedure lives in `docs/OPERATIONS.md` under Uninstall and is linked from
the README Install section. Commands are `arco service uninstall` and
`npm uninstall -g @ghreprimand/arco`. Optional deletion covers the Arco home
(`~/.arco`, `%USERPROFILE%\.arco`, or `ARCO_HOME`), a separate `ARCO_VOICE_HOME`
when set outside that home, per-project `.arco/` folders, and Git refs matching
`refs/arco/*` via `git for-each-ref` and `git update-ref -d`, with PowerShell
forms for Windows. Service paths match `scripts/packaging/services.mjs`
(systemd `arco.service`, LaunchAgent `arco-workbench.plist`, Task Scheduler
task and `service/task.xml`). Checkpoint ref cleanup matches
`server/src/workflows/purgeWorkflow.ts`. The in-app manual entry
`arco-uninstall` points at the same guide. Node.js and provider CLIs are left
installed.

Checked with `npm run docs:check`, `npm run test:docs`, `npm run audit:public
-- --tree`, and the help-docs and help-coverage browser suites. No provider
calls were made. Remaining: physical-host confirmation that the documented
paths match a real package install on macOS and Windows.

The Claude dot's dropdown (`UsageBar.tsx`) listed Native and CLI sign-in text, allowance meter, source, account and freshness lines, a checked time, a disclaimer and a CHECK ALLOWANCE NOW button above the Session and Weekly bars. It now shows a Claude title row with one small refresh button (`UsageTitle`, aria-label "Refresh Claude usage limits", aria-disabled while checking so focus is kept), then the Session and Weekly bars, or the meter's own message when there is no data. A stale snapshot with bars still shows its bars; no wording about it. The Codex dropdown shows a Codex title with the same refresh button and its usage rows; the account chooser appears only when extra Codex profiles exist, and the legacy browser source and its login sit under a collapsed "More". The Cursor dropdown shows a Cursor title, the included gauge and the token total with the overage note; the polled-not-live header, the in/out breakdown and the dashboard link are gone (the dot still opens the usage page). Tooltips use border-box with a width capped to the viewport so they fit at 360 px.

User-facing "Allowance" became "usage limits" in the dot title, header help, the Connections help, `docs/OPERATIONS.md` (section now "Claude usage limits"), `docs/PROVIDERS.md` and `docs/HTTP_API.md`; internal names (`claudeAllowance.ts`, the suite name) are unchanged. No data was removed from `/api/usage`.

Checked with updated `claudeAllowance`/`claudeAuthStatus` unit tests, a new `usageDropdowns.test.tsx` (Codex and Cursor titles, one refresh button, no removed text), docs check, and the `claude-allowance` and `claude-auth-status` browser suites (title and bars, one button, fit at 1280 and 360 px, refresh fencing unchanged). Remaining: the Codex "More" source options and account chooser are retained for capability parity.

## 2026-11-02 -- Windows service runs without a console window

The Windows logon task `arco-workbench` ran `node.exe` directly (`<Exec><Command>` in `scripts/packaging/services.mjs`), so a console window stayed open on the desktop streaming the server log and git warnings. `renderService('win32')` now registers `C:\Windows\System32\conhost.exe --headless <node> <arco.mjs> service-run <config>` (path from `SystemRoot`, every argument quoted). Options weighed: `conhost.exe --headless` gives the process a console with no window and no flash and has no dependency on other components; a wscript plus generated .vbs launcher depends on Windows Script Host, which VBScript's retirement from Windows 11 puts at risk, and adds a file to keep in sync; PowerShell `-WindowStyle Hidden` flashes a window; an S4U or "run whether logged on or not" principal changes the profile and desktop the providers see. Ending the task still ends the process tree, so stop and restart behave as before.

With no console, `service-run` on Windows now redirects `process.stdout` and `process.stderr` into `service.stdout.log` and `service.stderr.log` in the log directory (`ARCO_LOG_DIR` or `<home>/logs`), 5 MiB each with one rotated `.1` copy (`service-logs.mjs`), next to the structured `arco.log` the server already writes. `arco service logs` prints `arco.log` and, on Windows, any non-empty raw file under headers.

Existing installs: `refreshServiceDefinition()` (`arco service refresh`) rewrites an Arco-owned task only when the stored hash still matches the file, registers the new XML from a staged copy first and changes the file and hash only after `schtasks /Create /F` succeeds; a modified definition is never touched. `arco update` and UPDATE AND RESTART run it in a fresh process (the new code) between install and start, tolerating failure with a note; `service-run` also calls it at start so a 0.1.0 install updated by the old updater is moved too, effective at the next start (the first start after that update can still show the window once).

Checked with unit tests for the generated XML, refresh (rewrite, current, modified, failed registration, no service), stdout/stderr capture with rotation and the logs report, and the update ordering (stop, refresh, start; Linux unchanged); `npm run smoke:package` gained a Windows step that runs `conhost.exe --headless node` and checks the definition. Not run on a real Windows host: that no window appears, and end-to-end task start, are unverified.

## 2026-11-02 -- Repository locations show their child folders

Project locations that are Git repositories now show the repository itself and its non-hidden child folders in Home and the creator. A location can therefore contain other projects without hiding them merely because the parent has a Git marker. The default Docker single-repository mount remains a single project, keeping its source directories out of the project list. The workflow name defaults to the selected project's display name, including on Windows paths, and an explicitly edited name survives later folder selections.

`scanFolderProjects` in `server/src/projects.ts` includes the repository and safe immediate children, and treats Git directory and file markers alike. The container mount exception applies only to the Docker project root. `ProjectPicker` supplies its existing project list to the creator, where `projectNameForDirectory` resolves display names and recognizes Windows and POSIX separators. The Home and creator help and `docs/GETTING_STARTED.md` explain the behavior.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, unit (1,271 passed) and documentation suites, public audit, the full browser runner and the project-roots browser fixture. The fixture creates a synthetic repository location and checks Home, creator choices, its default name and a retained edit. The focused scan test initially failed by returning only the parent, then passed with child discovery; unit cases also cover the isolated Docker root and Windows path naming. Occupied browser ports required alternate-port reruns; the interrupt fixture also needed its preview URL set explicitly. Real Windows and macOS acceptance remains open.

## 2026-11-02 -- Detect providers for a new home

New Arco homes now enable Claude, Codex and Cursor when their existing sign-in is detected, and Ollama, LM Studio and llama.cpp when their endpoint or executable is detected. Strata and DeepSeek through Xolotl start disabled. An installed runtime may still require a running server or a model. Existing settings files, saved provider switches and homes with saved projects or chats keep the previous availability behavior.

At startup `initializeProviderSettings` writes the complete initial `enabledProviders` map only when no prior settings or saved-work markers exist. `initialProviders.ts` reuses connection-status probes and checks local executables on PATH. Connections permits a disabled provider to be checked or signed in; a successful explicit check enables it, while a failed check leaves it disabled. A completed CLI login triggers that check, and a verified Native login from Settings or Connections does the same; choosing an already signed-in CLI enables Claude as well. Routine status refresh and workflow recovery do not change provider switches. Settings help, Connections help, the manual and `docs/SETTINGS.md` describe the behavior.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, the isolated unit suite (1,273 passed), documentation checks, the public tree audit, the complete browser runner before rebasing, and the affected first-run and Connections fixtures after rebasing. The provider fixture checks a Claude-only home, local detection, old settings without a provider map, explicit saved values and unsuccessful checks. An initial build used an unavailable status field and was corrected; a recovery fixture needed the new explicit check route, while workflow-bound recovery was kept unchanged. A later rebase required the in-place Native sign-in to remain usable while Claude is disabled. The default browser port was occupied for eight suites, which were rerun on an isolated alternate port. Real macOS and Windows acceptance remains open.

## 2026-11-01 -- Confirm status-only director messages

A director sending a status-only message to another cluster member now receives a confirmation note before delivery. The note asks for the same message to be sent again with `confirm: true`; the confirmed body is delivered unchanged. Peer reports and operator-originated messages do not use this check. Questions, assigned task IDs, the documented instruction verbs and attached artifacts bypass it, keeping actionable messages flowing.

The shared `peerSend` operation applies the deterministic word check before deduplication and budget accounting, and logs a held attempt as `status_confirmation`. The held attempt consumes no message budget. A confirmed status resend can proceed even when the normal per-turn limit is full; a successful delivery is counted. Native and MCP `peer_send` expose the optional `confirm` argument through their respective schemas and the cluster HTTP route. No existing message body or recipient is rewritten. The Unreleased changelog records the behavior.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, the unit suite (1260 passed), docs checks, the public tree audit and the cluster-coordination browser smoke. The focused coordination tests cover the held status example, unchanged confirmed delivery, full budgets, questions, task IDs, artifacts, actionable rebase instructions, peer reports and operator sends. An overlapping unit run was terminated; rerunning the complete suite alone passed. No provider calls or credential copies were used. Review, landing and hosted cross-platform verification remain open.

## 2026-11-01 -- Recipient context on peer sends

A peer send now returns the recipient's current turn state, active board task and most recent message to the sender, including the reply's first line and age. The MAILBOX inspection dialog shows the same context for each pending message. The context is informational: it never prevents a send or alters delivery, and unavailable live state is reported as unknown.

The shared peer-send operation builds the context from the live workflow registry, the cluster's selected task board, the sender's pending mailbox and its received-message events. Both the Native tool and MCP route return the result. The mailbox peek route enriches each pending row, and the mailbox modal displays the facts beside the existing message and cancellation controls. Restricted members do not gain board access. The mailbox help entry and the Unreleased changelog describe the added information.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, the unit suite (1259 passed), docs checks, the public tree audit and the cluster-mail-visibility browser fixture, including the new mailbox context row. A targeted 14-test run additionally exercised the shared context builder and send result. The first browser invocation found its usual port occupied; an isolated Vite instance on another port passed the same fixture. No provider calls or credentials were used. Review, landing and hosted cross-platform verification remain open.

## 2026-11-01 -- Stated next steps need no approval message

Cluster coordination now tells members that silence approves a next step already stated in a report. A reply is needed to change that step, answer a question, or assign other work. Reports that continue without a reply end by naming the next step. This reduces status-only follow-ups while retaining explicit communication for changes and questions.

The guidance is added once in `buildClusterCoordination`, beside the existing send check, and is included for Director and Implementer roles at Thorough, Balanced and Lean levels. It does not change peer delivery, message guards, task-board status, or the operator's controls. Existing coordination settings and saved roles remain in place. `CHANGELOG.md` records the changed guidance under Unreleased.

Checked on Linux with `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:unit` (1258 passed), `npm run docs:check`, and `npm run audit:public -- --tree`. The cluster coordination test checks the exact text appears once for both roles at all three levels and follows the existing send check. No implementation or verification failures were encountered. The change is held for the planned release sequence; review, landing, and hosted cross-platform checks remain open.

Queued follow-ups retain their existing delivery order and cancellation controls.

Other transports do not claim mid-turn delivery. No queue or provider scheduling behavior changed.

## 2026-11-02 -- Claude status follows the selected transport; Claude sign-ins grouped

On a fresh Windows home Connections and Welcome showed Claude as "CLI connected" while Native, the default transport, had no credentials, so a first Claude chat would have used an unsigned-in transport, and "Native sign-in settings" opened Settings at the local endpoint cards with no Native control in view. `claudeConnection()` in `client/src/utils/claudeAuth.ts` now derives connected, needs-Native-sign-in and CLI-signed-in from the status and the selected transport (`settings.claude.transport`, default native). Native evidence that is missing or needs attention reads "Native: sign-in needed" or "Native needs attention"; unknown or absent Native evidence keeps the earlier CLI-only answer so older statuses and probe failures never lock anyone out. `anyPrimaryProviderConnected` takes the transport, so the Welcome Continue check, the startup gate and the Home status line share the rule.

The Claude panel in Connections explains that Native and CLI are separate sign-ins, offers SIGN IN FOR NATIVE (the existing in-app flow via `useClaudeNativeLogin`, with its link, paste field and cancel shown in place), "Use Claude CLI instead" when the CLI is signed in (saves `claude.transport: cli`), and a way back to Native. In Settings > Providers and models the Native row and the CLI row now sit in one CLAUDE group (Native first) before Codex, Cursor and the local endpoints. The request `claude-signin` (also used by the header chip) opens that group, scrolls to it, focuses its first control and flashes an outline (static under reduced motion).

Checked with unit tests for the derivation across native/cli by signed-in/not, the gate and the state text, the settings navigation tests, docs check, and the `connections` browser suite (new scenario at 1280 and 390 px: Native sign-in needed, Continue disabled, sign-in start, settings landing and order, switching to CLI enables Continue). No real sign-in was performed. Remaining: a real Windows run.

## 2026-11-02 -- llama-server is located on PATH on every platform

On a Windows install the llama.cpp provider reported "llama-server binary not found at /usr/local/bin/llama-server". `server/src/providers/llamacpp.ts` checked a fixed POSIX path in three places (catalog probe, version probe, live probe), and `llamacppManager.ts` spawned the bare name regardless of `ARCO_LLAMACPP_BIN`. The new `providers/llamacppBinary.ts` resolves the executable once: `ARCO_LLAMACPP_BIN` wins when set (a missing override is reported, never silently replaced), otherwise PATH is searched with `llama-server`, and on Windows with `;` separators, quoted entries and PATHEXT (`llama-server.exe`). The three probes and the spawn all use it. The message is now "llama-server not found on PATH (set ARCO_LLAMACPP_BIN)".

Checked with `llamacppBinary.test.ts` (linux and darwin PATH search, Windows PATH with PATHEXT and a quoted directory, override precedence, no assumption of the old fixed location), plus build, typecheck, the unit suite, docs check and the audit. Not run on a real Windows host with llama.cpp installed. `docs/PROVIDERS.md` wording updated.

## 2026-11-02 -- Quiet service checks during setup

Fresh package setup no longer displays a misleading service-manager error when the per-user service does not exist yet. On Windows, `arco setup` previously printed the Task Scheduler missing-task error immediately before creating and starting the task successfully. The equivalent launchd check on macOS had the same output behavior. Existing services and configuration retain the same ownership checks and installation behavior.

`scripts/packaging/services.mjs` now captures output from the second `schtasks.exe /Query` and `launchctl print` checks in `service install`, matching the existing pre-install checks. The create and bootstrap commands still use the normal service-manager output and report failures through `call`. Start, stop, status, update and uninstall behavior is unchanged. The fixture tests in `scripts/packaging/services.test.mjs` check both probes on both platforms and confirm that a failed Windows task creation still reports an error.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, the unit suite (1,260 passed), `npm run docs:check`, the public tree audit and the host-only `npm run smoke:package` with a collected native artifact and npm 12. The new fixture first failed because the second probe inherited output, then passed with the capture in place. The package smoke initially failed when invoked without its required artifact argument; supplying the collected artifact resolved that invocation. ## 2026-11-01 -- Continue fresh-worker batch numbering

Repeated ADD FRESH batches with the same typed name now continue after the highest numbered member already in the cluster. A single-worker request still uses the typed name unchanged, and unnamed workers retain their role-based numbering. Existing members and saved workflows are unchanged.

The cluster panel sends its existing `cluster_add_fresh_workers` request to `handleClusterAddFreshWorkers`, which computes the complete numbered batch before spawning. If a suffix would make a generated workflow name longer than 64 characters, the whole request is refused with `name_too_long`. The in-app name help, cluster manual and protocol reference describe the numbering. The Unreleased changelog records the fix.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, 1,256 unit tests, public-safety and documentation checks, and the ADD FRESH and contextual-help browser fixtures. The handler test exercises repeated requests, a gap in existing suffixes, punctuation in the typed name, and the length-limit refusal without spawning. The initial regression test reproduced duplicate `#1` and `#2` names; after the numbering change, a punctuation case exposed a missing regular-expression escape, which was corrected. Fixtures use a temporary home and synthetic cluster without provider calls.

Remaining: Director review and hosted platform checks. The change is intended for the release after the initial package tag.

## 2026-11-01 -- Release devlog copies resolve their links

The hosted Release run for the first tag failed in its help-registry check: the documentation link test reported a missing file for a link in the release entry of `devlog/2026-11.md`. `scripts/release.mjs` copies the changelog section into the new devlog entry, and the changelog's links are relative to the repository root, so from `devlog/` they pointed at a nonexistent `devlog/docs/...` path.

`relinkForDevlog` in `scripts/release/helpers.mjs` rewrites repo-relative inline links in that copy with a `../` prefix. Absolute URLs, mail links, fragments, root-absolute paths and links that already climb are left alone, and a second pass changes nothing. The changelog section, the tag message and the stub's other text are unchanged. The existing release entry's link in `devlog/2026-11.md` was fixed by hand, and a missing separator before the following entry was restored.

`scripts/release/helpers.test.mjs` covers the rewrite rules. `scripts/release/cli.test.mjs` now gives its fixture changelog a root-relative link and checks that the dry-run preview and the committed devlog archive carry the `../` form while `CHANGELOG.md` keeps the original. A dry run of `node scripts/release.mjs patch` in the checkout shows the rewritten link. Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, the unit suite, `npm run docs:check`, `npm run test:docs` and the tree audit. No tag was changed.

## 2026-11-01 -- Browser smokes resolve Vite file URLs on Windows

On Windows runners nearly every fixture-based browser suite timed out waiting for its page to become ready. The fixtures route requests through an allowlist that turned a Vite `/@fs/` URL back into a local path by slicing four characters off the URL. Vite serves a Windows file as `/@fs/<drive>:/<path>`, so the slice left a leading slash before the drive letter, the path check failed, the request for Vite's client environment module was aborted, and the page script never set its ready flag. Help coverage built malformed `/@fs` URLs for the same reason by joining the prefix to a native path.

`scripts/smoke/fs_url.py` adds `fs_url` (local path to `/@fs/` URL path, forward slashes, no leading slash before a drive letter) and `fs_path` (the reverse, for both POSIX and drive-letter forms). Twelve browser scripts now use it instead of hand-built prefixes and slices: cluster coordination, form controls, the three creator suites, auto-pickup, workspace rename, cluster mail visibility, add-worker identity, Claude auth status, compaction threshold and help coverage. No product code changed; the cause was in the harness only.

Checked on Linux by running all thirteen touched suites, which pass unchanged. Windows behavior is checked by the hosted workflow dispatch for the Windows runner. A hosted run on the Windows runner then passed 31 suites and skipped the 5 that need the Linux isolated-server harness. `ci.yml` therefore splits the former best-effort job: `browser-windows` runs on every push and pull request without `continue-on-error`, and gates the build, while `browser-other` is now macOS only and stays best effort (its Recall and Phosphor timeouts are a different cause). `CONTRIBUTING.md` describes the change. Remaining: macOS browser timeouts, and Windows coverage for the five Linux-only suites.

## 2026-11-01 -- Release v0.1.0

First public release. Arco installs as the npm package `@ghreprimand/arco` (`npm install -g @ghreprimand/arco`, then `arco setup`) on Linux, macOS and Windows with Node.js 24 and npm 12. The `arco` command covers `setup`, `start`, `service install|start|stop|status|logs`, `update`, `voice setup`, `version` and `doctor`; the per-user background service is a systemd user unit, a launchd LaunchAgent or a Task Scheduler logon task. The package carries prebuilt native modules for x64 and arm64 on all three platforms, so installation runs no install scripts. Docker remains the secondary isolated option and building from source is the contributor path.

Release controls: `scripts/release.mjs 0.1.0` takes the inaugural path (no version bump, empty Unreleased, seeded `v0.1.0` section) and creates this commit and the annotated tag; `.github/workflows/release.yml` builds the six native archives, assembles and audits `arco-release.tgz` with `npm run release:pack`, and attaches it to the GitHub Release. The npm staging job finds no package on the registry and skips itself; the first npm publication is made by hand from the release archive, after which trusted publishing through the `npm` environment stages later releases for approval.

Checked: hosted CI on the release base passed verify on Linux, macOS and Windows, all native package builds, package install and run on all three platforms, the Docker smoke and the Ubuntu browser suites; `node scripts/release.mjs 0.1.0 --dry-run` accepted the tree. The macOS and Windows browser suites remain best effort and are not part of this release gate.

Remaining: physical-host acceptance on macOS and Windows, the first trusted-publisher release, and the update path from this version.

### Added

- A local software workbench with project-aware agent conversations, saved and Incognito chats, terminals, files, Git, Preview, Review, Compare, Research, Handoff, Spawn, clusters, plans, Recall, Knowledge, Genesis, Cookbook and Media studio.
- An npm package (`npm install -g @ghreprimand/arco`, then `arco setup`) with the `arco` command and per-user background services on Linux, macOS and Windows, plus Docker with a persistent Arco home and explicitly mounted project folders. Public documentation leads with that package install and service; Docker is secondary; source builds are documented for contributors. The package supports setup, foreground start, service management, updates, diagnostics and local voice setup. Source checkouts retain `npm start` and `npm run dev`.
- Skippable Welcome setup (optional operator name, project locations, Connections), Project Home with open work, locations, pins, bookmarks, archive, missing-folder recovery and one-click resume, and Settings organized by task with a control-location map.
- Provider integrations for Claude, Codex, Cursor, Ollama, LM Studio and llama.cpp. Optional Strata and DeepSeek through separately installed external services that Arco does not distribute. New homes default to Claude Native and Codex app-server where those transports apply.
- Clusters with size-ordered templates, at most one director on new work, work-size hints for repeated roles, ROWS member cards by default, local runtime capacity for Ollama and llama.cpp, and connected-only creator launch. AUTO-PICKUP modes: Off (default), Rule-based (the recommended automatic mode), Smart Observe-only and Smart Automatic (advanced evaluator options with durable limits and session expiry).
- Waiting Codex and Claude Native questions surface as a named toast, cluster card highlight and tab mark until settled.
- Optional native voice setup (`arco voice setup` or `npm run voice:setup` in a checkout) with guided microphone testing, Computer voice operator and download notices; voice requires a native install.
- Optional GitHub release notices: in-app toast (**OPEN RELEASE** / **DISMISS**), Settings > Advanced with **CHECK NOW** and opt-out. `npm run arco:update` selects the newest stable release by default; `--ref` and `--main` select a tag or commit with confirmation. It handles Windows checkout path aliases, refuses local changes and rolls back on failure. Packaged installs use `arco update` with rollback; their services offer **UPDATE AND RESTART** on all three platforms. Linux source services also offer **UPDATE AND RESTART**.
- In-app manual and contextual help for reachable controls, public reference documentation, contributor and extending guides, third-party notices and GPL-3.0-only licensing.
- Numbered releases with this changelog and a local release command that prepares commits and tags without pushing.

### Changed

- New homes use Phosphor Electric Blue and Victor Mono at 110% interface scale; a saved theme or font pack is left alone. Interface text size is adjustable from 90% to 150%.
- Project `.arco/` state is ignored by Git by default; customized ignore files are preserved.

The [long-form release notes](../docs/releases/v0.1.0.md) describe installation,
provider boundaries, download sources, and platform acceptance status.

## 2026-11-01 -- Workflow actions move to Node 24 majors

Hosted runs warned that Node.js 20 is deprecated because `ci.yml` and `release.yml` pinned action majors that run on it. Every action in both workflows now uses a major whose `action.yml` declares `node24`: `actions/checkout@v7`, `actions/setup-node@v7`, `actions/setup-python@v7`, `actions/upload-artifact@v7` and `actions/download-artifact@v8`. The runtime Node version for builds stays 24.

Compatibility review: the upload and download actions only gained direct-file support and ESM in these majors, and download v8 errors on a digest mismatch by default; the workflows move gzip tarballs and use `pattern` with `merge-multiple`, which are unchanged. `setup-node` caches automatically only when `package.json` names npm as its package manager, which this repository does not; every `setup-node` step in `release.yml` still sets `package-manager-cache: false` explicitly so a release job can never restore a cache. `ci.yml` keeps its explicit `cache: npm` on the verify and browser jobs.

Checked by reading each action's `action.yml` at the chosen tag, parsing both workflows as YAML, and a hosted CI run on a temporary branch started with the workflow dispatch form for all three operating systems. The release workflow runs only on tags and was not executed; its changes are the same version substitutions plus the explicit cache setting. Remaining: the first tagged release is the end-to-end test of `release.yml` on the new action majors.

## 2026-11-01 -- Keep package audits stable across client builds

Native release package checks now accept the two reviewed minifier identifiers even when ordinary client changes alter the generated bundle. The package audit still reports unrelated email addresses and requires the exact reviewed SQLite binary for its binary false positive. This restores package install checks after changes to the in-app manual without excluding the generated client bundle from public-safety scanning.

`scripts/public-audit.mjs` matches the two reviewed token digests only in packaged `client/dist/assets/index-*.js` files. The Windows SQLite exception remains scoped to its native path, exact artifact digest and exact token digest. `scripts/packaging/public-audit.test.mjs` checks both tokens in changed bundle contents, an unrelated address in the bundle, a token outside that bundle, and a modified SQLite artifact. No runtime controls or settings change.

Checked on Linux with the build, typecheck, unit suite (1,257 passed), documentation checks and public tree and staged audits. The npm 12 package smoke uses six hosted native artifacts and a synthetic home, fixture service manager and local update packages. The new focused test failed against the prior artifact-wide client digest and passed after narrowing the exception to the reviewed tokens. No provider calls or credentials were used.

Remaining: the three hosted package-install jobs must confirm the correction; the separate real macOS and Windows acceptance and manual review of public artifacts remain open.

## 2026-11-01 -- Release workflow publishes through the npm environment

The `stage-npm` job in `.github/workflows/release.yml` now declares a GitHub environment named `npm` with the URL `https://www.npmjs.com/package/@ghreprimand/arco`. Nothing else in the workflow changed: the job still needs `release`, keeps `id-token: write` and `contents: read`, downloads the `arco-release-package` artifact and runs the same steps.

The reason is visibility and trust scope. A job that names an environment is recorded under the repository's Deployments, so each release run shows the package URL it staged. The environment name is also what an npm trusted publisher matches: the publisher entry for the package on npmjs.com must name the workflow file `release.yml` and the environment `npm`, otherwise the OIDC token from the job is not accepted. The environment is created automatically on first use; protection rules can be added in the repository settings without changing the workflow.

The first release is published to npm by hand, so staging must not run, and must not show as a failed deployment, before the package exists. A new `npm-package-exists` job runs after `release` and asks the public registry with `npm view @ghreprimand/arco name` (no credentials). An E404 sets `exists=false` and logs a notice that staging was skipped because the first publish is manual; success sets `exists=true`; any other error fails that job. `stage-npm` needs both jobs and has a job-level `if` on the output, so it is skipped rather than failed on the first tag. The GitHub Release job does not depend on either.

Checked by parsing the workflow as YAML and confirming the job's environment mapping, the `if` condition and the unchanged step list, plus the tree audit, `npm run docs:check` and the unit suite on Linux. The release job itself was not run, so the first real release after this change is the end-to-end test of the trusted-publisher match. Remaining: configure the trusted publisher with environment `npm` on the registry before the next release.

## 2026-11-01 -- Stage native releases with trusted publishing

Release tags now stage the audited npm package through trusted publishing while the GitHub Release is created independently. A missing or misconfigured npm trusted publisher reports a staging error without blocking release notes and the package asset. The initial package can also be assembled for manual publication before the trusted publisher is configured.

`.github/workflows/release.yml` runs the npm staging job on a hosted runner with OIDC permission and no npm token; its Node setup does not cache dependencies. The assembled package records the repository URL required for provenance. `npm run release:pack` combines the six collected native artifacts into the same audited tarball used by the workflow. `scripts/packaging/README.md` describes collection, assembly and the one-time manual publication.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, 1,256 unit tests, documentation checks, public tree and staged audits, and the npm 12 package install smoke. The release-pack command assembled and audited the six native artifacts from hosted checks; its manifest contained the required repository URL. A workflow structure check verified independent release and staging jobs and the staging permissions. An initial pack attempt preceded completion of the client build and passed after the build finished. The package smoke exercised a temporary home and fake service manager without provider calls.

Remaining: configure the npm trusted publisher and verify staging on an actual release tag; real macOS and Windows acceptance and Director review remain open.

## 2026-11-01 -- Install and update the native workbench package

The package provides `arco` for foreground use, setup, services, updates, voice setup, version and diagnostics. It carries platform-specific Node 24 bindings without depending on install scripts. State stays in the Arco home, and source checkouts retain their launch and Git update commands.

`scripts/packaging/assemble.mjs` packages the compiled workbench, shared workspace and native licenses. Native collection verifies platform, ABI and hashes. `arco service` uses systemd user units, launchd agents or Task Scheduler logon tasks, retaining the Node executable and install-time settings. `arco update` refuses linked checkouts, snapshots the package and restores it on failure. Services offer UPDATE AND RESTART; foreground installs show a terminal command. `arco setup --repair-native` rebuilds incompatible bindings in the Arco home.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, 1,256 unit tests, documentation and public audits, and update-action and help-coverage browser suites. npm 12 installs passed with scripts disabled and with defaults in temporary prefixes. SQLite, terminal, manual references, fake services, local-tarball updates and rollback were exercised. Compiled test files were removed from the tarball. A registry-dependent rollback was replaced by a local snapshot. An early probe read an existing unit without changing it; subsequent checks require package ownership and use fixtures. A callback-port collision cleared on rerun; Windows fixtures isolate process checks and shim invocation.

Native collection removes build paths from unsigned Windows debug and diagnostic fields, omits unused signed console binaries, and strips macOS debug metadata before an ad hoc signature and runtime probe. Hosted checks exposed a non-executable macOS helper and Windows tar path handling; both were corrected. The package audit pins reviewed media copies and minifier and SQLite false positives by digest.

Remaining: hosted checks, Director review of audit exceptions, real macOS and Windows acceptance, and npm setup. Provenance requires a public repository.

## 2026-11-01 -- Resolve Windows checkout aliases during updates

Windows updates can identify the checkout when Git and Node report the same directory using different short names, separator styles or letter case. The health endpoint uses the same checkout identity, so the updater can still detect a running server before replacing native modules. Linux service detection also compares resolved checkout paths consistently.

`scripts/arco-update.mjs` resolves checkout paths with `realpathSync.native`, normalizes Windows separators and case, and applies the result to the checkout-root check, service working-directory check and checkout fingerprint. `server/src/http/appVersionRoutes.ts` computes the health fingerprint with the same rules. The updater fixture injects a short-name alias and differing slash and case spellings, checks fingerprint equality, and retains case-sensitive comparisons on Linux. No update controls or saved state changed.

Checked on Linux with `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:unit` (1,233 passed, none skipped), the public tree audit, and the focused updater suite (19 passed). The previous hosted Windows suite failed at the checkout-root check for every updater fixture; native resolution and normalized comparison address the reported mismatch. A first audit of the synthetic Windows path flagged a home-directory pattern; using a neutral fixture path resolved that finding. Temporary local repositories and injected runners were used, with no provider calls or live service changes.

Remaining: a hosted Windows run must confirm the correction before the first release tag; real Windows and macOS acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-01 -- Update to a stable release from the workbench or terminal

The updater selects the newest stable release tag, checks out its commit, installs dependencies and builds. Linux user services can also use UPDATE AND RESTART after confirming the release and the interruption of running turns. Other run modes show a copyable command, including host-side Docker commands. A result notice reports completion or rollback after reconnect.

`npm run arco:update` uses the dependency-free `scripts/arco-update.mjs`. CHECK, dry-run, explicit refs, `--main`, confirmation and downgrade controls keep terminal updates reviewable. The command refuses tracked edits, unpushed local commits and target collisions with untracked or ignored files. It preserves the service unit, rolls back failed updates and checks Windows native-module locks before changing the checkout. The browser endpoint only accepts confirmation of the server-checked latest release and uses the restart authorization boundary. A separate transient user unit keeps the updater outside the service being restarted.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, 1,232 unit tests, documentation and public-safety audits, and the update-action and help-coverage browser suites. Temporary Git origins, synthetic tags and injected npm and service runners exercised refusals and rollback without touching a live service. A Windows-style clone with line-ending conversion verified checkout without forcing changes. An initial unit run found two help entries missing from the Settings navigation map; adding them resolved it. No paid provider calls or credentials were used. Remaining: hosted Windows and macOS checks, a real Linux user-service acceptance, and the inaugural release tag.

## 2026-11-01 -- Name and role when adding a fresh cluster worker

ADD FRESH in the cluster panel (`AddWorkerSection.tsx`) now has two optional fields above COUNT. NAME is used as typed for one worker and gets ` #1`, ` #2` and so on for several. ROLE offers the member-row presets as toggle buttons plus a custom text field; DIRECTOR is disabled when the cluster already has a director or when more than one worker is spawned. `cluster_add_fresh_workers` carries optional `name` and `role` (`shared/src/protocol/clusters.ts`).

`handleClusterAddFreshWorkers` in `server/src/ws/clusterHandlers.ts` validates both before anything is created: name length (64, or 60 when several), role type, and the one-director rule against the cluster's current roles and the requested count. A refused request spawns nothing. The role is stored through the same role-set path as a member row. With an empty name, workers are called after the role label ("Builder #1"), the first 24 characters of a custom role, or "Worker #1"; numbering skips suffixes already used by the same base. Previously every fresh worker copied the template member's name, which could yield "Director #3" with no role. ADD EXISTING, REPLACE, work size and focus are unchanged.

Help entries `cluster.fresh.name` and `cluster.fresh.role`, the cluster manual, `docs/PROTOCOL_CLUSTER.md` and `CHANGELOG.md` describe the fields. `server/src/ws/clusterAddFreshIdentity.test.ts` covers typed names, role-derived names, custom roles, numbering, a refused second director with no spawn, over-long names and several directors in one request. The new `add-worker-identity` browser suite checks the request payload, the resulting member card with its role chip and the disabled director preset. Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, unit suite, audits, `docs:check`, help coverage and the cluster browser suites. Remaining: macOS and Windows acceptance.

## 2026-11-01 -- Public audit refuses a tracked state directory from the upstream project

`scripts/public-audit.mjs` now reports `upstream-state-directory` for any tracked path with a segment equal to the state directory used by the upstream project (a dot followed by its name), at any depth. The check sits in `inspectFile`, so it runs in the tree, staged, release-history and push scopes. History scopes apply it too, unlike the plain-name check, because runtime state under that directory is never publishable at any revision. The directory name is built from parts, as the existing upstream-name check does, so the tracked tree stays free of the literal.

This complements the existing `local-runtime-state` rule, which covers `.arco`, `.codex` and `.claude`. Findings carry the path and rule name only.

Two cases in `scripts/public-audit.test.mjs` cover both directions: a force-added file under a nested state directory fails in the staged scope and, once committed, in the history scope; an ignored untracked state directory passes the tree scan. Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, the unit suite, tree, staged and release-history audits, and `npm run docs:check`. Remaining: macOS and Windows acceptance.

## 2026-11-01 -- Project state ignored by Git by default

Project `.arco/` contents are now ignored by Git by default, including plans, task boards, knowledge, project settings and workflow files. A project can explicitly share a file by adding an exception such as `!settings.json` to `.arco/.gitignore`. A parent ignore rule can still exclude the directory, and files already tracked by Git remain tracked until removed from the index. Force-add also remains possible.

`server/src/projectStateIgnore.ts` creates the project ignore file with `*` before state writers create their files. Workflow initialization, plan and board stores, knowledge, project settings watchers, MCP configuration and other project state writers use the same helper. An existing ignore file is upgraded only when its contents exactly match the earlier default; customized files are preserved. A non-regular ignore file prevents workflow initialization rather than allowing unprotected state to be written. `docs/PERSISTENCE.md` and `docs/OPERATIONS.md` explain the default and exceptions.

Checked with isolated temporary projects and `git check-ignore`, including a settings exception, exact legacy upgrade and edited-file preservation. The first Git fixture inherited a machine-wide ignore rule and obscured the exception; isolating the fixture's Git exclude configuration resolved it. Workflow initialization failure and retry, first plan, board, knowledge and settings watcher paths are covered. On Linux, build, typecheck, 1,205 unit tests (none skipped), documentation and public-safety audits passed. First-run and project-roots browser suites passed, as did Genesis scaffold (50 checks), Genesis rebind (3 steps), knowledge (7 steps) and session index (25 checks). No provider calls were made. Remaining: hosted macOS and Windows verification.

## 2026-11-01 -- Public audit rejects tracked agent instruction files

The repository no longer tracks `AGENTS.md`, and `.gitignore` ignores the common agent instruction file names and the `.claude/`, `.codex/` and `.cursor/` directories. The earlier override that force-included `AGENTS.md` is gone. The file stays on disk in the checkout.

`CONTRIBUTING.md` carries the contributor essentials in plain terms: the local gate commands, what the hooks in `.githooks` enforce, the devlog entry format and the public-safety rules. References to the removed file are gone from it and from `docs/REPO_LAYOUT.md`, and the documentation link checker no longer lists it. Product behavior that reads a user's project instruction files (trust gate, TUNE MD, Genesis ignore rules) is unchanged.

`scripts/public-audit.mjs` gains two checks. `agent-instruction-file` fails when any of those names is tracked, in the tree, staged and history scopes. `typographic-punctuation` is an ASCII-punctuation check on added Markdown lines in the staged scope and on commit messages; existing content and non-Markdown files are not rescanned. Three new cases in `scripts/public-audit.test.mjs` cover each check both ways, including an ignored untracked file and an unchanged legacy line.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, unit suite, tree and staged audits, and `npm run docs:check`. Remaining: macOS and Windows acceptance stay open.

## 2026-11-01 -- Documentation style guide

Tracked guides and the in-app manual now share an explicit style guide. Authors open each page with purpose, then prerequisites, then content typed as concept, task, reference or troubleshooting. Dated history stays out of the current contract. Density rules favor short paragraphs, one-fact bullets, numbered steps with fenced commands, tables for comparisons, bold for UI labels only, code font for commands and paths, and ASCII punctuation in impersonal voice.

The guide lives in `docs/STYLE.md` and is linked from `docs/INDEX.md` under Start here and Project. It also states loss-prevention checks: keep facts unless shown stale, keep a fact checklist when rewriting, and run documentation and public-safety checks as required by the contribution process.

Checked with `npm run docs:check` and `npm run audit:public` for the tree and the staged diff in the docs-readability worktree. No CHANGELOG bullet: this adds authoring guidance only. Remaining: apply the style to README Install and Getting started, then Features, Voice, the remaining guides and the in-app manual, one coherent commit at a time.

## 2026-11-01 -- Guarded Rule-based AUTO-PICKUP

Rule-based AUTO-PICKUP can now wake idle cluster members without a model while skipping acknowledgement-only batches and limiting repeated automatic turns. Alternating wakes between two members pause after a configurable count; operator messages, NUDGE, re-arming or third-member mail clear that pair pause. A rolling hourly member cap and an arming total bound further wakes. RESUME starts a fresh arming total. Off remains the default, and saved mode choices remain intact.

The cluster AUTO-PICKUP dialog recommends Rule-based and places Smart modes under Advanced. Its Rule-based limits set the alternating-pair count, hourly member cap, arming total and chain depth, with RESET LIMITS. Defaults are four, six, 40 and eight respectively. `server/src/clusterAutoTurn.ts` applies the existing cooldown and per-minute ceiling alongside the new guards. The manual, `docs/PROTOCOL_CLUSTER.md`, `docs/SETTINGS.md` and `docs/GOTCHAS.md` explain these controls. Rule counters and pair pauses are stored in the cluster ledger and survive a server restart.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, 1,200 unit tests (none skipped), documentation and public-safety audits, and the AUTO-PICKUP and help-coverage browser suites (2 passed). Focused tests cover acknowledgements, mailbox admission, pair clearing, persisted budgets, resume and a busy admission race. An initial build lacked development dependencies; installing them with `npm ci --include=dev` resolved it. An older Smart test expected a depth cap of three; updating it to eight restored the unit gate. No model or provider calls are needed for Rule-based pickup. Remaining: real-host macOS and Windows acceptance.
## 2026-11-07 -- Align client and API references with current behavior

The client and API references now describe the current request paths and saved cluster focus. They also explain that media files outside a project require an explicit folder allowance.

`docs/CLIENT.md` names the request-response HTTP surfaces alongside the persistent workflow WebSocket, records that `clusterFocusedMember` is persisted only for a member still in its cluster, and fixes two stale documentation references. `docs/PROTOCOL.md` describes the protocol directory without a file-count estimate. `docs/HTTP_API.md` matches the media access fence: project and scratch paths remain available, while outside-folder paths require enabled access and an allowed root. `docs/OPERATIONS.md` explains that 0.1.4 can still read legacy cloud-snapshot blobs, while snapshots written by 0.1.5 need a compatible hub.

Checked the settings and media access implementation in `appStore.ts`, `settings.ts`, `mediaOpsRoutes.ts`, and `MediaAccessSettings.tsx`. `npm run docs:check` and `node scripts/public-audit.mjs --tree` are run for this documentation change.

The initial read found a contradictory persistence statement and an API access rule that described outside-folder access as open by default. Both references now match the implementation. The rollback guide also lacked the hub's new checksum-named blob compatibility limit. No runtime behavior changed.

Remaining: complete the line-by-line review of the other partially read client, protocol, HTTP API, gotchas, and provider references. Real macOS and Windows acceptance remains a separate release requirement.
